市場焦點
美國經濟
• 第三季GDP初值季增年率為 3.4%，高於市場估計的 3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。美國9月個人消
費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，年增3.6%；核心PCE指數月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局 ，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局 、市場對
於AI投資前景的疑慮持續 ，可能加大短期股市波動風險 。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存 ，
持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次 ，
將提高川普後續推動政策的難度 。觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期 ，期中選舉的隔年
通常是股市表現最佳的年度 ，其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後 ，政治不確定性降低 ，市場對

政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
中國
• 9月工業企業利潤年成長 14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快 ，中國工業利潤改善 ，主要由AI
硬體與能源價格上漲帶動 ，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企業則是能
源價格上漲的最大受益者之一 ，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線 ，但內需疲
弱與價格競爭問題未解，投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。

美國第三季GDP成長超預期、通膨壓力續增
• 美國第三季經濟成長率(GDP)創一年來最大增幅：美國經濟分析局(BEA)公布，第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場
估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。企業AI投資火熱、消費者開支保持韌性，抵銷了油價上漲衝擊。
• 9月數據顯示通膨壓力續增 ，但符合預期：美國9月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，寫3月以來最大月增幅；年增幅
3.6%。剔除食物與能源的9月核心PCE指數，月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 市場反應：由於新一輪通膨數據符合預期 ，市場未改變聯準會 12月利率決議升息一碼的預測 。然而，有跡象顯示實質個人
消費支出開始轉弱，儲蓄率也有所下降，市場開始押注通膨可能已經見頂。從聯邦利率期貨市場數據來看，市場對於明年升

息空間的預期已較上個月收斂(九月底預測升息2~3碼→目前預測1~2碼)，
• 觀點：美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，
可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
資料來源：Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.30。

顧
聯準會貨幣決議與聲明稿差異
資料來源：Federal Reserve（10/28、9/16 聲明）；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。以上為聲明重點整理。
2 / 5
10月決議｜按兵不動
3.75% – 4.00%
10票贊成、2票反對
經濟措辭轉強 「穩健擴張」改為「強勁擴張」；國內支出具韌性，生產力與資本投資表現強。
通膨仍未解除 聲明維持「通膨居高」的判斷，未再宣稱本次升息有助加快回到2%目標。
政策訊號更具彈性 改稱需要更多資訊判斷適當政策立場，未承諾下一步升息或降息。
關鍵變化
理事庫克(Lisa D. Cook) 和克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth M. Hammack)主張本次升息1碼，
形成2張鷹派反對票；9月決議則為一致通過。利率不變不等於轉向寬鬆，委員會內部對通膨風險仍

有不同判斷。
市場解讀：本次是「暫停觀察」；12月是否再升息，仍取決於通膨、需求與能源價格。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
資料來源：Federal Reserve 10/28會後記者會；Reuters、Investing.com，2026/10/28。
3 / 5
01｜市場利率反映緊縮壓力
華許指出，9月與 10月兩次 會議之
間，美國公債名目與實質殖利率明
顯走高；市場利率可先行反映成長
、通膨與風險變化，不必等政策利
率調整。
政策含意
長天期殖利率仍受通膨預期、期限
溢酬及公債供需影響；政策利率不
變，不代表債市壓力解除。
02｜AI投資與生產力
華許認為美國經濟尚未過熱，AI投
資推升需求，但生產力也有改善；
聯準會將持續評估新科技對產出、
就業與物價的中長期影響。
政策含意
AI支出可支撐企業成長，也伴隨資本
支出回收與融資風險；應檢視現金流
、投資報酬及生產力是否實際提升。

03｜政策制度與工具再檢視
聯準會規劃2027年安排1次、2028
年安排2次專注貨幣政策策略的會
議，不進行利率表決或發布政策聲
明；另將檢視資產負債表政策。
政策含意
制度調整不等於政策轉向寬鬆；仍須
觀察準備金、資產負債表操作及經濟
數據，評估金融條件如何變化。
解讀：華許除談利率外，也聚焦市場利率、AI生產力及政策制度調整；投資判斷仍應回到數據、企業獲利與金融條件。

聯準會利率決議摘要暨投資策略建議
資料來源：Federal Reserve；Reuters、InvestingLive；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。市場價格可能隨後續交易更新。
4 / 5
政策決議 利率維持 3.75% – 4.00% 經濟強、通膨高；政策下一步保留彈性
美股
短線受利率預期與科技股評價牽動
；AI投資支撐獲利，但仍需檢視現
金流與資本支出回報。
美債
若升息預期升溫，短天期殖利率較
敏感；長天期亦受通膨預期、發債
供給與期限溢酬影響。
美元／黃金
美元可能隨利差預期波動；黃金同
時受實質利率、美元及地緣政治避
險需求影響。
投資策略建議｜情境式配置
股票 保留核心持股，避免追價；優先關注獲利能見度、現金流與定價能力較佳企業。
債券 以分散到期日與信用風險為主；可分批配置高品質債券，避免單押長天期利率下行。

多元資產 保留流動性與再平衡空間；若通膨再升、殖利率上行，分批進場並控管整體波動。
後續觀察：美國通膨與就業數據、能源價格、企業財報，以及市場對12月政策的重新定價。

美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政
資料來源： 各媒體，2002~2022年
歷屆期中選舉確認結果時間
選舉日期 眾議院控制權確認時間 參議院控制權確認時間 時長
2006年11月7日 選舉日當晚 11月9日 2天
2010年11月2日 選舉日當晚 11月3日 1天
2014年11月4日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2018年11月6日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2022年11月8日 11月16日 11月12日 8天
• 美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政：美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，為川普第二任期上任以來首場全國性選
舉，除國會議員改選外，亦包括州長及其他公職人員選舉。若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，將提
高川普後續推動政策的難度。不過，川普仍可透過發布行政命令等方式，在一定程度上繞過國會的立法制衡，持續推動其政

策議程。
• 本次選舉結果可能耗費一週才能明朗：回顧2006至2022年近五屆美國期中選舉，其中四屆的參眾兩院控制權於投票當晚或
隔日即大致明朗，僅2022年因關鍵席次競爭激烈、郵寄選票計票耗時較長，直至投票後第8天才確認兩院席次歸屬。考量本
次參議院選情膠著，加上川普執政以來美國政治極化持續加劇，不排除本次選舉結果需耗費一週才能明朗，期間市場也可能
面臨額外的不確定性。

期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度
• 期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度：觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳
的年度。根據1940至2025年統計，標普500指數第三年平均報酬率達16.5%，高於第一年的8.3%、第二年的4.2%及第四年
的6.9%；中位數報酬率更達18.9%。其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，
且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
• 政黨與國會組成影響有限，基本面仍是關鍵： 進一步觀察政黨差異，1940年以來，第三年由共和黨總統執政時，標普500指
數平均報酬率約18.2%，高於民主黨總統執政時的14.9%，但兩者均呈現正報酬，顯示總統所屬政黨並非決定股市表現的唯

一因素。國會組成亦可能影響政策推動與市場預期，例如共和黨總統搭配民主黨國會時，第三年平均報酬率約15.6%；若國
會呈現混合執政，平均報酬率則達23.1%。

能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升
• 能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升：9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快。但前三季度累計增速
從1到8月的15.7%小幅放緩至15.5%，利潤改善主要反映了能源和 AI硬體價格的上漲，其中AI硬體價格仍是高技術製造業利潤
的重要支撐。前三季度，高技術製造業利潤年成長52.0%，與1到8月54.7%的增速大體相當。拉動整體利潤成長約10個百分點，
貢獻率約三分之二。值得注意的是，利潤成長仍主要集中於少數相關行業 ，其他企業部門整體依然處境艱難 ，內需疲弱疊加價
格競爭激烈令利潤受到擠壓。
• 投資建議：中國工業利潤改善 ，主要由AI硬體與能源價格上漲 帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企

業則是能源價格上漲的最大受益者之一 ，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線 ，但內需疲弱與價格競爭問題未解 ，
投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.27
能源、AI價格飆升提振中國相關行業利潤增速

中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
• 中國科技公司享受負殖利率差 ：中國在經濟成長放緩與寬鬆貨幣
政策下，借貸成本持續下降 ，10年期公債殖利率由年 初1.86%降
至1.63%，使企業融資條件更加寬鬆 ，相較之下 ，美國受經濟韌
性與高利率政策影響，10年期公債殖利率升至約5%，企業整體融
資成本明顯提高 。此外2025年中國大型科技企業在境內債券市場
融資規模有限 ，僅為約74億美元，而且支付的殖利率 ，平均比同
風 險 層 級 的 債 券 高 出18 個 基 點，但現在 溢 價 已 經 變 為 折 價，
2026年迄今，中國科技企業已在境內 發債約100億美元，新發科
技債殖利率平均折價6個基點，顯示機構投資者信心發生了顯著轉
變，大幅降低了行業推進 AI建設的資金成本 ，會有上述現象是 由

於中國經濟通縮，難以承受更高利率 ，因此官方須維持較寬鬆的
金融條件 ，科技企業剛剛開始通過債券市場融資 ，預料債券將受
到投資者關注。
• 展望後市： 低成本債券融資對中國科技行業構成利好 ，與IPO 不
同，債券市場為企業提供了一個可重複利用的融資渠道 ，在股市
劇烈波動之際 ，對投資人是另一種資產配置 ，可優先關注具資金
優勢與應用場景的大型科技龍頭 ，以及受惠於 AI算力需求成長的
伺服器、網通設備和資料中心產業。

中美國債走勢分化

AI產業鏈融資警訊：資金缺口正在擴散
• 資本支出與現金流的嚴重失衡：AI生態系中的巨額投資正考驗著企業，一旦企業營運現金流不足以支持基礎設施建設，就必
須高度依賴外部融資(發債或增資)，使其更容易受到資本市場波動與利率變化的衝擊 。AI算力提供商資金缺口最為顯著 ，其
資本支出已接近營運現金流3倍(284%)，需要投入大額資金採購高端GPU並對外出租算力，數據中心資本支出佔現金流的比
例已從2022年11月(ChatGPT問世)1倍(104%)快速爬升至1.3倍(128%)，超大規模雲服務商資本支出占現金流比例已從2022
年底40%翻倍飆升至73%。在彭博研究統計的8家超大型雲業者中，出現自由現金流為負(資金缺口)公司數量已從2022年的
1家增加至4家(達50%)。

• 產業鏈錯綜複雜，風險具連鎖效應： 從上游晶片製造商 、硬體開發商、建築商、電力/公用事業，到中游的數據中心與雲端
服務商，再到下游的軟體與數據管理公司，其銷售與支付合約彼此高度緊密相連。當算力提供商或數據中心出現資金吃緊時，
風險會迅速向上游傳遞，導致項目延期、融資成本攀升，甚至延後整個生態系的收入實現時程。目前除了雲端與數據中心外，
硬體開發商與建築商中出現資金缺口的企業比例也在上升。
資金缺口正蔓延至人工智能產業鏈一半的層級
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
算力提供商和數據中心的資本支出超過現金流

市場定價轉變：借貸成本上升與股票估值修正
• 資本市場已經開始針對資金缺口進行風險定價：在資金缺口最大的AI算力提供商、數據中心以及超大規模雲服務商中，其發
行的公司債相較於美國國債的殖利率差 (利差)顯著拓寬，高於一般投資級債券或高收益債券的表現 ，顯示債券市場對其違約
與融資風險要求更高的風險補償。由於大量現金流被基礎設施建設掏空，且AI應用端的獲利回收速度未達市場預期，投資人
不再願意給予極高溢價，整體AI產業鏈的預期本益比出現明顯修正。
• 小結與投資建議：當前AI產業正在經歷基礎設施過渡期，資本密集的算力出租、數據中心建設正遭受強烈的資金擠壓，若未
來1~2年內下游應用端的商業化營收無法順利銜接，依賴高度槓桿與外部融資的企業將面臨嚴峻的流動性挑戰 。對AI算力出

租商及部分高負債的數據中心開發商保持謹慎態度，一旦信貸市場收緊或借貸成本升高，利潤與營運將受重創。晶片製造商
的經營現金流持續成長，財務結構改善，選擇擁有強大訂價權與現金流護城河的上游半導體巨頭，安全邊際最高，電力與公
用事業資本支出佔現金流比例相對穩定且受惠於剛性需求。超大規模雲服務商中表現差異大，優先選擇資產負債表健全、資
產回報率高且擁有傳統核心業務能持續提供穩定現金流的科技巨頭，遠離需大量靠發債維持資本支出的個股。
超大規模雲服務商和數據中心的利差擴大
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
隨著借貸成本波動加劇，AI股權估值走弱

顧 • 利率可能維持高檔更久，但收益價值正在浮現：近月以
來美國 10年期公債殖利率一度升抵 5.36%，30年期接
近5.7%，反映市場重新評價未來利率中樞 、財政赤字
及資金需求等因素。雖然長天期債券價格仍可能受到利
率波動影響，但高殖利率已大幅提高新進場資金的收益
起點，投資焦點應由預測利率轉向評估收益價值。
• 債券標售與資金流顯示配置需求逐步回溫： 10月美國
財政部10年期、20年期及30年期公債標售需求維持穩
健，投標倍數約介於2.5至2.8倍區間；同時，美國債券
基金已連續 26週呈現資金淨流入 ，顯示市場未必認為
利率已見頂 ，但已開始接受 5%以上殖利率的長期配置
價值。
• 收益優先取代押注利率轉折： 在Higher for Longer環
境下，債券的核心功能重新回到穩定收益、分散風險與

降低組合波動 。建議可以 3至7年期美國公債及高評等
投資級公司債作為核心收益部位，長天期債券則採分批
布局方式，兼顧收益來源及未來利率循環變化帶來的潛
在機會。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。
警 語

CHPT
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and the structural shift towards high-end
testing. Our target price of NT$4,350 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the company's average forward PE
since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical

integration model. With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play
(rather than a consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin

compression from lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated US AI GPU and ASIC programs;
3) intensifying competition in testing boards from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

## Document 097: 261027_daiwa_ardentec

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_ardentec.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Ardentec released its 3Q26 results and held its investor meeting after market 
hours on 27 October. EPS of TWD3.1 beat our estimate (TWD3.04) and the consensus 
(TWD2.89) by 2.1% and 7.3%, respectively. Operating profit was broadly in line with our 
forecast, but beat consensus by 5%, driven by the Longtan plant ramp-up and higher 
utilisation rates.

We have a Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD395, based on target PER of 25x on our 2027E 
EPS.

What’s the impact:

• Stronger-than-expected 3Q26 gross margin: Ardentec’s 3Q26 revenue reached 
TWD5,337m (+17.7% QoQ, +48.5% YoY), broadly in line with both our estimate and the 
consensus. The strong revenue growth was primarily driven by volume ramp-up at the 
Longtan facilities and supported by spillover demand. Gross margin came in at 45.4% 
(+5.2ppt QoQ, +9.7ppt YoY), beating our forecast and consensus expectations by 0.3ppt 
and 1.7ppt, respectively, mainly due to the Longtan plant ramp-up and higher utilisation 
rates (approximately 80%). Operating income was TWD1,860m (+35.0% QoQ, +112.8% 
YoY), largely in line with our estimate, but beating the consensus by 5.1%. Net profit of 
TWD1,473m, largely in line with both our estimate and the consensus. Although 3Q26 
EPS came in at TWD3.1, beating our estimate and the consensus by 2.1% and 7.3%, 
respectively, the variance was mainly due to share-dilution timing instead of operational 
issues.

• 4Q26 guidance largely in-line: Management guided for 4Q26 revenue to be flat or 
slightly up QoQ on traditional seasonality, which is in line with our forecast (up 3% 
QoQ). On margins, management guided for a slight QoQ decline in gross margin, 
mainly due to higher labour cost and depreciation expenses, which is also consistent 
with our forecast. We estimate 4Q26 gross margin at 44.0%, down 1.4ppt QoQ.

• Solid demand for 2027: For 2027, we expect Ardentec to continue benefiting from the 
AI-driven growth trend, supported by (1) robust wafer sorting outsourcing demand from 
tier-1 foundry customers and (2) strong AI networking demand from a major U.S. 
networking customer. The company is currently engaged in several co-packaged optics 
(CPO) projects with leading U.S. industry players, which could provide it long term 
opportunities and a larger addressable market.

New capacity in expansion: The company continues to expand its production scale to

to diversify the global production sites, improve the product delivery capabilities, and 
supply elasticity.

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 35.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,188. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部維持聯亞買進投資建議，主要考量：(1)3Q26 營收及獲利皆
優於市場預期；(2)AI Data Center 矽光需求持續強勁，主要客戶
訂單能見度超過 6 個月，4Q26 營收可望續增，1Q27 新增產能開出
將帶動營收明顯跳增；(3)管理層維持先前 2027 年產能擴增指引
(+150%YoY)，並且確立2028 年產能擴增速度與 2027 年相當，亦即
兩年擴增產能超過 6 倍。惟考量(1)2026 年新增後段產能驗證時間
較研究部預期長；(2)3Q26 毛利率及費用率優於預期；(3)2027 年
產能增長幅度優於研究部預期 ， 因此調整2026/2027 年營收分別為
55.2 億元(原估 55.92 億)及 155.6 億元(原估 150.4 億)，EPS 分別
為 20.95 元(原估 19.96 元)及 58.76 元(原估 54.06 元)。目標從
3,800 上修至 4,000 元，重申買進投資建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收、EPS均優於市場預期，矽光出貨帶動獲利成長： 
聯亞3Q26營收15.62億元(+27.9%QoQ，+182.6%YoY)，略低研究
部預期，主因為後段產能驗證時間較研究部預期長 ； 營收雙增
主要還是受惠AI Data Center 矽光產品出貨量持續增加。毛
利率58.6%(+1.1pts QoQ，+15 pts YoY)，營業利益為7.71億
元，營益率達49.4%；營業費用1.43億元(+11%QoQ，+4%YoY)，
主因為研發及員工分紅費用增加；稅後淨利 6.21億元，EPS 
6.12元， 優於研究部預期 ， 主因為毛利率及費用控管優於研究
部預估。產品組合方面，Data Center占營收80–85%，其中以
矽光產品為主 ；Telecom占5–10%， 其他約5%， 與上季比重相當。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異 
營業收入 1,562 1,596 -2.1% 
營業利益 771 713 +8.1% 
稅後淨利 621 575 +8.0% 
EPS(元) 6.12 5.65 +8.0% 
資料來源： 聯亞財報、統一研究部預估

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 4Q26營收持續季增，1Q27新增產能帶動出貨加速： 
展望4Q26，主要客戶目前訂單期間已超過6個月，管理層表示
在手訂單金額已超過2026年全年營收規模， 顯現出目前需求
仍 然 十 分 強 勁 。 管 理 層 預 期 營 收 季 增幅 約 與4Q25 相當
(+16.2%QoQ)，即18.15億元 。 然而 ， 依照過往管理層給予的指
引通常較為保守，且在需求強勁以及新加入的 合作夥伴陸續
開出 後 段 產 能 下 ， 研 究 部 預 估 4Q26 營收為 18.32 億元
(+17.3%QoQ，+185.2%YoY)，EPS為6.77元。

展望1Q27，隨相關設備導入及後段委外產能開始量產，管理層
預期1Q27產出量將有更明顯增幅 。研究部預估 1Q27營收為
275.2億元(+50.2%QoQ，+204%YoY)，EPS為10.26。

3. 美國客戶四年約加持，2028年產出目標為2026年的6.25倍： 
聯亞8M26與美系Data Center 客戶簽訂2027–2030年為期四年
的CW Laser供貨長約 ， 客戶並以保證金方式提供資金支持 ， 以
確保未來產品供應 。 為履行長期供貨承諾 ， 公司同步追加近30
億元資本支出計畫，使過去一年累計核准資本支出超過 80億
元 ， 預計2027年產出顆數較2026年增加150%，2028年再較2027
年增加150%， 相當於2028年產出規模達2026年的6.25倍 ， 其中
8月新增資本支出主要貢獻於2028年。此外，為確保擴產後的
原料供應，公司亦與兩家日系及一家美系InP基板供應商簽訂
長期採購合約 ， 並持續增加台灣及海外後段委外代工夥伴 。 研
究部認為，美系客戶透過長期供貨合約及保證金支持聯亞擴
產 ， 顯示客戶對未來CW Laser需求具有高度確定性 ， 亦有助提
高新增產能的去化能見度，支撐公司2027–2028年營運成長。

4. 多波長CW Laser具技術及ASP優勢，NPO較CPO先行： 
聯亞800G及1.6T矽光產品已進入量產，分別具備8顆雷射及8
至16顆不同波長雷射的解決方案。管理層指出，多波長產品
ASP較單波長高出50%以上，且全球具備大規模量產能力的供
應商有限 ， 有利公司維持產品競爭優勢 。 隨未來光通訊傳輸規
格持續升級，公司亦積極開發3.2T及NPO/CPO應用所需的高功
率CW Laser。其中，管理層預期NPO有望於2H27率先作為過渡
方案，目前所接觸客戶以外部光源設計為主；CPO則因光源配
置 、 連接器等相關規格尚未完全定案 ， 大規模導入時程仍有待
觀察。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

此外，隨NPO/CPO等應用對光源功率要求提高，未來單顆 CW 
Laser功率提升至250–300mW以上 。 管理層指出 ， 高功率產品因
晶粒面積增加及良率下降，相同InP基板產能可產出的晶粒顆
數可能減少30–50%，使高功率光源對基板及有效產能的需求
增加 。 研究部認為 ， 短期聯亞仍以既有CW Laser產品擴產及多
波長方案出貨成長為主要動能，中長期則隨 NPO/CPO逐步導
入 ， 高功率光源有望成為新增需求來源 ， 並提高相關產品的製
造及供應門檻，將有望成為其技術護城河，維持競爭優勢。

5. Data Center高速PD完成驗證，預計隨新增產能逐步量產： 
除CW Laser外，聯亞亦持續布局Data Center高速PD產品。管
理層指出 ，200G以上PD已完成客戶驗證 ， 預計2027年新增產能
陸續開出，開始量產 Data Center PD 產品。惟目前仍以 CW 
Laser為主要生產重心，因此PD產出規模相對有限，但有助於
降低產品集中度風險。

表二、研究部預估調整

資料來源： 統一研究部預估

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對創意的投資建議維持強力買進，係因:(1)3Q26 營收、毛利
率、營業利益率皆優於預期，(2)4Q26 雖因為 ECB 將送件未給財測，
但客戶對 CPU 需求來看仍持續上修 ，(3)北美車用晶片有望在 2Q27 初
導入 ， 中國車用客戶晶片市佔率持續提升 ， 且持續拓展海外客戶 ，27-
28 年貢獻幅度大幅跳升 ，(4)晶片大廠持續上修 CPU 與加速器的配適
性，更有效率的使用 GPU 之效能，(5)不僅 200 系列晶片，CSP 客戶
的 300 系列晶片創意也有所著墨。綜合以上，上修創意 26 年 EPS 預
估為 63.3 元(原估 54.76 元，+125%YOY)，同步上修 27 年 EPS 預估
為 245.9 元(原估 214.5 元，+288.5%YOY)， 及上修28 年EPS 為 467.7
元(原估 392.6 元，+95.9%YOY，以股本膨脹 3%計算)。考量 27 年下
半年創意的成長動能更加明確上修 ， 且費用控管十分優異 ， 客戶從雲
端 AI 延伸至終端 AI，CPU、加速器、自駕晶片及 CPO 皆有著墨，故
對創意看法更加樂觀，故先前 8600 元目前價到價後，仍維持強力買
進投資建議，目標價調升至 9850 元(27 年 EPS*40XPER OR 28 年
EPS*21XPER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅優於預期，OPM 更加優化：  
創意 3Q26 營收達到創新高且優於上修後的預期的 187.5 億
元，成長35%QOQ，成長117.7%YOY，其中量產營收達到166.7
億元，成長44%QOQ，推估來自於CPU 的貢獻較大，且在2H26
創意也有新增一些產能支援 ， 且NRE 也達到預估的區間上緣 ，
僅衰退 11%QOQ。 毛利率達到20.59%， 優於研究部預期的19%，
更優於市場預估的 19-20%，推估主要是出貨的晶圓良率更加
提升所致。費用控制非常良好，3Q26 費用僅 13.09 億元，低
於 2Q26 的 13.18 億元，但營收卻成長35%QOQ，故使得OPM 提
升至 13.6%，大幅優於先前預估的11%。稅後淨利達到創新高
的 22.73 億元(原估 16.02 億元)，成長 46.1%QOQ，成長
162.1%YOY，EPS 16.96 元(原估 11.96 元)。

2. 因 ECB 將送件，故無 4Q26 財測，但仍正向看待：  
因為創意的 ECB 即將送件，故此次財報後無法給出財測，但
研究部仍認為 4Q26 創意營運動能將上修，主要係因先前在上
半年所提到的投片保有量外，仍有多爭取的 WAFER，從創意
3Q26 每個月的量產營收就可以觀察到持續增加，單月量產營
收已經提升至 65 億元，預估 4Q26 將能持續穩定攀升。另外
NRE 方面，CSP 下一代 CPU 將預估在 4Q26 TAPE OUT，NRE 預

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

估將翻倍成長 。 綜合以上 ，因為量產營收持續優於預期， 故上
修 4Q26 營收為 244.2 億元(原估 236 億元)，成長30.2%QOQ，
成長 97%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為續創新高的 30.1 億元(原
估 25.35 億元)，成長32.5%QOQ，成長159.6%YOY，EPS 22.46
元(原估 18.92 元)。

3. CPU在Agentic AI世代重要性持續提升： 
根據INTEL的資料中心與人工智慧白皮書顯示，CPU 容量不足
可能造成編排卡頓、工具執行延遲、驗證循環變慢，導致GPU
在等待控制平面 （代理編排） 處理完成時利用率不足 。 若要將
代理型工作負載最佳化，就必須增加 CPU 數量，作為控制與
編排層，在兼顧 新式 AI 工作流程分散且多步驟本質的同
時，將資料高效提供給加速器。故根據INTEL實測顯示，在模
型大小為8B規模時 ， 建議加速器與CPU配比為1.4:1， 模型大小
擴大到70B時，建議配比為1:1。故在AMD簡報也顯示，2025-
2030年CPU產值規模將從260億美元提升至2030年的2200億美
元，CAGR達到50%。創意的CSP客戶所ASIC走到第八代後，CPU
與TPU的配置從先前1:4確實提升至1:2，並且全數從先前的
X86 BASE更改成自家研發的ARM BASE CPU。而CSP客戶第八代
產品放量時間點將落在4Q26末，2027年完整貢獻 ， 故從創意量
產營收看出 ， 因為配比提升CSP客戶對CPU需求持續增加 ， 故上
修CPU26年貢獻創意營收從12億美元上修至13-15億美元。在
27年方面，有鑑於客戶對於加速器及CPU的配飾性相對於第七
代提升一倍，加上下一代CPU將在4Q26 TAPE OUT，有望在2H27
開始貢獻。因為二代產品單價提升，故上修CPU貢獻營收程度
從35億美元上修35-40億美元。 
CSP二代CPU將有望在28年完整貢獻，ASP提升幅度較大，且三
代CPU目前已經開始著手研發 ， 故研究部認為 ，CPU對加速器的
配置開始往1:1走後，創意目前在客戶需求延續性強，且持續
有拿到次世代案件下，28年CPU貢獻幅度將達到50%YOY增長。

圖一、INTEL 資料中心白皮書內容

資料來源：INTEL，統一研究部整理

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

圖二、AMD 對 SERVER CPU 產值的預估

資料來源：AMD，統一研究部整理

車用將成為 27 年開始的新成長支柱 
(1)北美客戶： 
根據供應鏈訪查 ， 北美車用客戶晶片量產時間點將落在2Q26，
創意目前在 5 代晶片進度仍優於競爭對手，故以目前規劃的
投片量來看 ， 研究部維持先前預估貢獻約12 億美金營收不變 。
但根據北美客戶法說，該客戶希望將5 代晶片在 2027 年 4 月
導入機器人使用 ， 故研究部目前僅估算車用的部分 ， 尚未估計
機器人，若該客戶順利量產機器人，則貢獻創意營收有望上
修。 
(2)中國客戶： 
以中國客戶自行揭露的資料來看，全系列征程系列晶片出貨
套數已經高達 1000 萬套，在 26 年 9 月，該客戶的 CEO 在社
群媒體上表示，公司智駕晶片出貨量已達 1500 萬套。另外客
戶給出 26 年征程系列晶片將出貨 500 萬套，27 年將更達到
700 萬套的目標。先前預估 27 年以客戶出貨目標 700 萬套的
50%，350 萬套第 7 代征程晶片做估計，貢獻營收將達到 15-
20 億美元，此預估沒有變化，靜待客戶完成年報公告後再行
調整。

圖三、中國自駕車晶片公司 CEO 微博

資料來源：WEIBO，統一研究部整理

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

兩大車用客戶對創意來說 ，27年僅是開端 ， 首先北美客戶27年
才會導入5代晶片，且是機器人及無人計程車皆會導入，附帶
一提，先前展示的飛天跑車也是搭載5代晶片，故認為在客戶
量產機器人及無人計程車後，晶片需求在 28年將會超過翻倍
成長 。 另外在中國客戶方面， 除了本土市場外 ， 該公司積極拓
展海外市場，7代晶片LIFECYCLE有望比6代更久，28年目前也
以翻倍的狀況預估。

4. CSP客戶三代晶片仍有著墨機會： 
北美另一雲端客戶在10月初表示，200系列加速器已經實現量
產，該客戶已經在各地區DATA CENTER 部屬200系列晶片，而
200系列晶片後段為創意打造，推估LIFE CYCLE出貨量為數十
萬顆 ， 貢獻營收約3-5億美元 。 而在300系列方面 ， 先前市場傳
出主要供應商為美國晶片同業 ， 後來研究部訪查 ， 創意在27年
亦有BOOKING 2nm產品，而300系列晶片由N2P節點打造，故推
估創意亦有部分300系列晶片相關生意，故仍有營收貢獻。而
目前推估400系列晶片有望重回創意，挹注 28年後的營運動
能。

5. 26、27年上修預估，28年持續高度成長： 
創意3Q26營收已經大幅優於預期 ， 加上毛利率擊敗市場預期 ，
另外費用控管得宜 ， 使得3Q26 OPM不減反增 ， 另外4Q26雖然未
給財測，但在產能有所支持加上CPU需求持續推升下，營收也
略有上修，故上修創意26年營收預估為685.2億元(原估648.6
億元)，成長100.7%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為84.8億元(原估
73.28億元)， 成長125%YOY，EPS上修預估為63.3元(原估54.76
元)。 展望27年，CPU貢獻性持續上修 ， 另外也維持車用客戶的
預估值，另外本來就有CSP客戶的200系列加速器開始大幅量
產，300系列目前有所著墨，故上修27年營收預估為2458.5億
元(原估2328億元)，成長258.8%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為
329.5億元(原估287.5億元)， 成長288.5%YOY，EPS上修預估為
245.9元(原估214.5元)。最後，雖然目前28年投片等尚未確
定 ， 不過創意在CSP二代CPU、 中國及北美車用晶片 ， 接續世代
晶片皆有持續獲得新世代案件 ， 另外在CSP ASIC方面 ， 目前有
將從整合封裝能力開始切入 ，CSP 300/400系列晶片皆有斬獲 ，
故研究 部粗估28年創意營收將上修預估為 5145.8億元(原估
4627.5億元)，成長109.3%YOY，稅後淨利預估上修為645.5億
元(原估542億元)，成長95.9%YOY，EPS預估為467.7元(原估
392.6元，以膨脹後3%股本計算)。

強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上

8422 可寧衛(Cleanaway)

報告日期： 2026/10/22 研 究 員：洪  國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 高雄 205 兵工廠專案整治案，己於 2024~2025 年分別認列營
收金額 2 億元與 12 億元，初期主要以開挖為主，掩埋次之，
預期進入中後期掩埋高峰階段，預估 2026~2027 年營收金額
10 億元與 10 億元。  
 小港焚化新爐興建，在 BOT 會計制度，總興建成本 350 億元，
工程期間 3.5 年，在興建期間需認列工程成本當作營收認列，
毛利率為零 ， 宏遠推估新爐2026 年貢獻營收金額 25 億元 ，2027
年貢獻營收金額 40 億元。  
一. 預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 0.33 元 
在 BOT 興建工程認列金額較 2Q26 減少因素，可寧衛 3Q26 營收
金額 19.89 億元 ，YoY+88.45% ，QoQ-12.29% ， 預估營業利益5.28
億元， YoY+44.72% ，QoQ-4.47% ，預估稅後盈餘 4.46 億元，
YoY+58.37% ，QoQ-2.79% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.33 元。  
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 0.37 元 
在 4Q26 興建工程進入高峰，預估可寧衛 4Q26 營收金額 22.09
億元， YoY+122.16% ，QoQ+11.04% ，預估營業利益 6.23 億元，
YoY+78.56% ， QoQ+17.95% ，預估稅後盈餘 5.09 億元，
YoY+82.15% ，QoQ+14.12% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.37 元。  
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.25 元 
可寧衛 2026 年雖然在 205 兵工廠整治工程認列金額減少 ， 但2026
年 205 兵工廠營收認列主要以高毛利掩埋比重為主，帶動整體獲
利呈現小幅成長。預估可寧衛 2026 年營收金額 75.80 億元，
YoY+58.99% ，預估營業利益 20.87 億元， YoY+7.54% ，預估稅
後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.25 元。  
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 1.35 元 
可寧衛 2027 年在 205 兵工廠掩埋處理量小幅增加，加上大承再
生粒料廠貢獻全年度營運，預期可寧衛 2027 年營運將持續成長。
宏遠預估可寧衛 2027 年營收金額 96.00 億元， YoY+26.65% ，預
估營業利益 22.97 億元 ，YoY+10.08% ， 預估稅後盈餘18.35 億元 ，
YoY+8.24% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.35 元。  
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 34 元 
觀察可寧衛歷史 PER11~30X ，2026 年 ROE 16.9% ，PER 21X ，
2027 年 ROE17% ，PER 19.3X 。評價偏低，故投資建議買進，目
標價 34 元（ 25X2027 年稅後 EPS ）。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
可寧衛成立於 1999 年，經營有害事業廢棄物之處理業務，資本額
13.59 億元，主要業務包含有害重金屬污泥廢棄物固化清理、鋼鐵
業集塵灰固化清理、都市垃圾焚化爐飛灰固化清理、有害石綿廢
棄物固化清理與非法棄置場址整治業務，可寧衛為全國最大一站
式開挖、清運、固化、掩埋垂直整合之合法環保公司。可寧衛旗
下有五間子公司，其中岡聯事業以廢棄物清運為主、大倉實業以
一般事業廢棄物掩埋場、可寧衛企業以固化物專屬掩埋場、吉衛
以固化物專屬掩埋場、可寧衛投資主要負責中國大陸業務為主。

圖一、可寧衛集團

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二. 現行廢棄物處理方式與市場概況  
廢棄物主要分成一般廢棄物與事業廢棄物，一般廢棄物主要以家
庭垃圾為主，在經過分類回收後，將進行焚化再中間處理，其中
可寧衛為最終掩埋廠。事業廢棄物在經過資源回收，再區分成有
害廢棄物與一般事業廢棄物，經過焚化處理後，再進行中間處理
（固化處理），最終進入掩埋。可寧衛為最終的固化處理與掩埋。

三.可寧衛公司經營模式與客戶概況  
可寧衛主要客戶分兩大類，第一類為製程客戶，如鋼鐵廠、焚化
爐、電子公司、汙水處理廠等，生產過程中排放出來的廢棄物，
如汞、鋅、銅或其他金屬，首先交由岡聯事業清運，分出一般廢
棄物與有害廢棄，如果是一般廢棄物由大倉事業負責掩埋，若是
有害廢棄物則先做固化處理 ， 再交由可寧衛企業/吉衛來負責掩埋 。

第二類客戶為汙染清理客戶，主要處理非法棄置場址與關廠及土
水法列場址，可寧衛歷年來在關廠廠址業務有新竹飛利浦竹北
廠、桃園日商太平洋玻璃廠、台塑前鎮廠、南亞林園 DOP 廠、中
石化前鎮廠、台電興邦獅甲段、台肥前鎮廠、高雄榮民工程等。

圖四、可寧衛服務流程

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

四. 2026 年營運概況及 2027 年展望  
營收來源主要分成製程客戶與汙染清理客戶。在製程客戶營收淡
旺季主要需視客戶淡旺季而定， 1Q 在工作天數與下游客戶出貨量
較少情況下，為傳統淡季，之後逐季走高至 3Q，而後 4Q 將會略
為下滑。在汙染清理客戶方面，主要視案量大小與時間長短而定，
無明顯淡旺季之分。

可寧衛營收結構包括廢棄物固化及開挖、廢棄物掩埋、廢棄物清
運服務、再生紙銷售、預拌混凝土銷售、太陽能發電與其他共七
項。可寧衛 2025 年營收概況，廢棄物固化及開挖 2025 年營收金
額 17.71 億元， YoY-18.7% ；廢棄物掩埋 2025 年營收金額 15.8
億元 ，YoY-10.0% ； 廢棄物清運服務2025 年營收金額 1.44 億元，
YoY+37.1% ；再生紙銷售 2025 年營收金額 4.30 億元，
YoY-35.0% ；預拌混凝土銷售 2025 年營收金額 2.39 億元，
YoY+131.6% ；太陽能發電 2025 年營收金額 5.75 億元，
YoY+63.1% 。

廢棄物固化及開挖 2025 年營收金額 17.71 億元， YoY-18.7% 。觀
察營收衰退主因在於高雄中油煉油廠專案整治工程帶動
2023~2024 年廢棄物固化及開挖營收維持在相對高檔水準。 2025
年僅有 205 兵工廠專案，且開挖與固化比重相對較少因素。預期
2026 年，在 205 兵工廠專案整治工程減少，加上國內石綿瓦專案
固化金額未能彌補 205 兵工廠專案 ， 預估廢棄物固化及開挖2026
年營收金額 8.20 億元， YoY-53.7% 。預期 2027 年，在石綿瓦專
案逐漸結束，固化處理量減少，預估廢棄物固化及開挖 2027 年營
收金額 7.50 億元， YoY-8.5% 。

再生紙銷售主要以上評資源為主。上評資源主要產品為各類牛皮
紙，瓦楞芯紙，工業管用紙，特殊用紙及植生資材之製造及分條
加工。再生紙銷售 2025 年營收金額 4.3 億元， YoY-35.0% 。預估
再生紙銷售 2026 年營收金額 1.7 億元， YoY-60.5% 。預計 4Q26
結束再生紙業務， 2027 年無營收貢獻。

預拌混凝土銷售主要以吉鼎材料為主。吉鼎 CLSM 廠收受再生粒
料，經過混凝土預拌製程，製程控制性低強度回填材料 (CLSM) ，
使用於道路基底層，低密度再生透水混凝土等，將廢棄物產品化，
拓展廢棄物一貫處理流程。吉鼎己於 2Q~3Q24 正式商轉，貢獻
2024 年營收金額 1.03 億元。 2025 年貢獻全年度營運，預拌混凝
土銷售 2025 年營收金額 2.39 億元，YoY+131.6% 。預期 2026 年
第二條產線配合大承再生粒料 4Q26 正式投產，預估預拌混凝土
銷售 2026 年營收金額 3.2 億元， YoY+34.1% 。預期 2027 年在搭
配大承開始投產，帶動預拌混凝土出貨量持續增加，預估預拌混
凝土 2027 年營收金額 4.2 億元， YoY+31.3% 。

太陽能發電主要以大創綠能為主 ， 大創綠能為可寧衛持股 100% 孫
公司。大創綠能主要位於高雄彌陀區設立綠電共生廠，將分三期
規劃，其中第一期裝置容量 69MW ，第二三期裝置容量
53~60MW 。第一期將投入 40 億元建置， 05/2024 開始營運，貢
獻 2024 年營收金額 3.52 億元。在 2025 年貢獻全年度營運，太
陽能發電 2025 年營收金額 5.75 億元， YoY+63.1% 。預期 2026
年，在發電量小幅增加，預估 2026 年太陽能發電營收金額 7.20
億元， YoY+25.3% 。預估 2027 年，在平均單價小幅增加，太陽
能發電營收金額 7.50 億元， YoY+4.20% 。

在其他營收方面， 2026 年將開始貢獻營收，包括高雄 AI 智能高
效焚化爐 BOT 案、大承再生粒料廠。

可寧衛 06/03/2025 公告取得 「高雄市AI 智能高效焚化爐 BOT 案
(小港 )」之最優申請人，可寧衛與台灣人壽成立 SPV 公司，其中
可寧衛持股 55%， 台灣人壽持股45%。 高雄小港焚化爐 ，BOT 案，
舊爐年處理量 34 萬噸，興建新爐年處理量 42 萬噸，營收來源包
括新焚化爐售電收入，新焚化爐代操作與舊焚化爐代操作，其中
新爐將於 2025 年下半年開始興建， 2028~2029 年正式營運，新
爐營運期 20 年。原有焚化爐將有代操作營運，己於 4Q25 正式移
交， 1Q26 開始小幅運作， 2Q26 正式營運，宏遠推估舊爐 2026
年處理量 22~26 萬噸，每噸操作收入 1700~2000 元，宏遠預估小
港焚化爐代操作貢獻 2026 年營收金額 2.5~3 億元（可寧衛持股
55% 進合併營收）。貢獻 2027 年營收金額 3~3.5 億元。

新爐興建方面，在 BOT 會計制度，總興建成本 350 億元，工程期
間 3.5 年，在興建期間需認列工程成本當作營收認列，宏遠推估
新爐 2026 年貢獻營收金額 25 億元， 2027 年貢獻營收金額 40 億
元，上述營收之毛利率為零。

大承再生粒料廠，可寧衛 100% 持股孫公司。大承再生粒料廠主要
使用固化及乾燥製程處理無機污泥 /灰渣 &有機污泥 /無害油泥等製
程再生粒料及資源化燃料，可收受代碼共 35 種（ D 類， R 類， S
類），月處理量 9000 噸，4Q26 末正式營運，預估 2026 年處理量
4.5~6 萬噸，每噸處理費用 6000~8000 元，宏遠預估大承再生粒
料貢獻 2026 年營收金額 0.5~1 億元 。 預估大承再生粒料貢獻2027
年營收金額 5 億元。

可寧衛能源，可寧衛持股 75% 。可寧衛能源主要將在桃園觀音地
區設立固體再生燃料  (SRF) 發電廠，投入 30~35 億元興建， 2022
年底動工。 SRF 發電廠是以回收塑料與廢紙、木材等生質物轉製
成的燃料來發電，再生燃料除了碳排放量大幅低於煤炭外，也能
作為工業鍋爐燃料使用，用途範圍廣泛。可寧衛能源主要營收來
源有兩部分，處理費與售電收入。

可寧衛能源回收塑料與廢紙等生質物，日處理量 350 噸，推估年
處理量 10 萬噸，每噸處理費用 7000 台幣，推估每年營收金額 7
億元。售電收入：再生燃料發電廠裝置容量 17MW ，全年發電度
數 1.2 億度，扣除自用 15% ，每度電 4.74 元計算，年營收金額
4.8 億元 。 宏遠預估2Q~3Q27 正式營運 ， 保守原則先不列入2027
年營收貢獻中。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

預估 2026 年稅後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，稅後 EPS 1.25
元

可寧衛 2026 年雖然在 205 兵工廠整治工程認列金額減少 ， 但2026
年 205 兵工廠營收認列主要以高毛利掩埋比重為主，帶動整體獲
利呈現小幅成長。宏遠預估可寧衛 2026 年營收金額 75.80 億元，
YoY+58.99% ，預估營業利益 20.87 億元， YoY+7.54% ，預估稅
後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.25 元。 (以 1
股分割 10 股後股數計算 )

預估 2027 年稅後盈餘 18.35 億元， YoY+8.24% ，稅後 EPS 1.35
元

可寧衛 2027 年在 205 兵工廠掩埋處理量小幅增加，加上大承再
生粒料廠貢獻全年度營運，預期可寧衛 2027 年營運將持續成長。
宏遠預估可寧衛 2027 年營收金額 96.00 億元， YoY+26.65% ，預
估營業利益 22.97 億元 ，YoY+10.08% ， 預估稅後盈餘18.35 億元 ，
YoY+8.24% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.35 元。 (以 1 股分割 10 股後股數
計算 )

## Document 280: Australian dwelling approvals_ units lift the headline (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian dwelling approvals_ units lift the headline (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-03

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
APPROVALS BULLETIN
Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ 
1.710 điểm và số mã còn giữ xu hướng tăng từ đáy tháng 7 suy gi ảm khiến xu 
hướng ngắn hạn trở nên trung tính.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

- Sau khi phủ sóng các thành phố lớn, chuỗi Long Châu đang mở rộng sang các vùng cấp 2, 3. 
Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm

Vietnam’s economic success story has continued

◼

projected to slow in the short term, reaching a still strong 
 level of

, but

have 
 deteriorat

in recent months. Public and external debt ratios

moderate.

 , 
but

than expected  
so far
 . Economic 
activity

 strong

deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

Earnings above all 
• Higher oil prices are in a tussle with stronger growth. We expect strong growth 
to help the global economy weather inflation risks, allowing equities to focus on the 
strength of earnings growth. Oil prices remain the key risk to monitor 
• Maintain a preference for equities over bonds and cash.  Strong earnings 
growth means we would use the recent pause to add exposure to our preferred 
markets and themes. The US and Asia ex-Japan remain our preferred regions 
• Take advantage of higher bond yields via credit risk but cap duration. The 
risk of higher bond yields looks most pressing in ultra -long maturities, but credit 
quality remains strong. We are Overweight EM USD bonds 
Hits from the 1990s 
Rising bond yields have been a key market theme.  Since mid -year, 10 -year 
government bond yields have risen approximately 40-80bps across the US, Japan and 
Euro area. Against this backdrop, gold has delivered high single -digit returns, while 
global equities have delivered low single-digit returns. 
Higher oil prices are in a tussle with robust growth data . The lack of progress 
towards a negotiated settlement in the US -Iran conflict and rising fears of shipping 
restrictions extending to the Bab al-Mandab Strait have meant that oil prices are once 
again testing the USD 100/bbl level. These worries have been exacerbated by 
increasing worries about refined product (eg. diesel, jet fuel, etc) supply. Despite these 
concerns, US growth data has held up remarkably well, as illustrated by the latest PMI 
and retail sales data. This is underpinned by AI investment growth.  
Real, rather than nominal, energy prices are key.  A chart of inflation -adjusted 
energy prices, though, illustrates why the global economy has managed to hold up 
despite these risks. Oil prices in real (inflation-adjusted) terms are closer to long term 
averages rather than at true ‘oil shock’ levels. This, together with the global economy’s 
lower oil intensity relative to history, is why we expect  growth and inflation to avoid 
worst-case scenarios as long as nominal oil prices avoid an excursion into the USD 
120-150/bbl range.

The Fed is likely to hike twice more by mid -2027. The 
acceleration in AI-driven investment growth means we believe 
the chances of a shift from our core soft-landing to no-landing 
economic scenario have gone up. This, combined with still -
high inflation readings mean we now expect the Fed to hike 
rates twice more by mid -2027. In contrast, the BoJ and ECB 
are expected to hike thrice and once more respectively. 
Buy-the-dip in equities 
1990s rate hikes a better parallel then 2022 . There is 
understandably some concern about whether rising central 
bank policy rates, and rising bond yields across maturities, will 
present a challenge to equity markets. However, we believe 
rate hikes in the 1990s are a better historical parallel, when 
central banks were hiking rates into strong economic and 
earnings growth, resulting in equity markets maintaining their 
pace of gains. The 2022 hiking cycle, in contrast, represented 
an inflation surprise, which we believe is not the case today. 
Strong earnings growth overwhelms other factors today. 
Still-strong economic and earnings growth means that we 
remain constructive on the outlook for equities and other risky 
assets going into year -end and beyond. The pause over the 
last couple of months was likely driven by a combination or 
rising bond yields , September seasonality and excessive 
investor optimism around mid-year.  
However, earnings expectations for global equities remain 
very strong (especially in our preferred US and Asia ex-Japan 
regional markets). We believe these earnings remain 
sufficiently robust to outweigh the drag from higher bond 
yields. Therefore, w e remain Overweight equities relative to 
bonds and cash, though we believe it is prudent to ensure the 
size of the equities Overweight is not excessive. Within Asia, 
we also trim China equities to a core holding, leaving Taiwan 
as our only regional Overwei ght, given the ongoing focus on 
AI investment-driven markets for now. 
Bumps remain part of the journey. As we laid out in our mid-
year outlook, the second half of this year is expected to be 
characterised by positive, but bumpy returns as a result of the 
factors laid out in the previous section, but also US mid -term 
election risks. The second half has thus far played out as 
expected given sufficient bumps, but still robust earnings 
growth. We would not be surprised by some volatility around 
US mid-term elections, but any further pullbacks or pauses 
would be opportunit ies to raise exposure given strong 
earnings growth is expected to ultimately dominate. 
Prefer credit over duration 
Corporate credit quality remains strong . Aggregate 
indicators of credit quality suggest credit quality remains 
strong across both Investment Grade and High Yield bonds 
across most regions. US IG bond yields face some upward 
pressure from significant hyperscaler supply, but supply -
driven spread widening tends to be relatively short -lived in 
nature and should not signal significant credit quality worries. 
Longer-maturity bonds remain at risk of further volatility. 
In contrast, we continue to be concerned that the volatility in 
very long maturity bonds could extend. Continued inflation 
and debt worries mean it is possible investors continue to 
demand a greater ‘term premium’ (ie. a premium for holding 
bonds with very  long maturities). We continue to believe 
limiting bond duration to 3-7 years remains appropriate.  
We continue to prefer  corporate and EM over government 
bonds. This implies earning a higher yield by taking on more 
credit risk (via corporate or EM bonds) rather than adding 
more duration risk. Within bonds, we remain Overweight EM 
USD bonds and view IG and HY bonds as core holdings. 
Modest USD weakness; gold to grind higher 
USD bounce led by pricing of Fed hikes . However, we 
believe Fed hikes are now excessively priced. At the same 
time, we see room for non-US rates to rise further, especially 
in Japan where the undervalued JPY is likely to strengthen. 
This is why modest USD weakness is likely to resume. 
Gold to continue grinding higher. While the move higher is 
likely to be characteri sed by two -steps-forward-one-step-
back, we believe an ultimately softer USD and continued EM 
central bank demand should result in continued gains in the 
precious metal over both the next 3- and 12-month periods.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

With the Federal Reserve’s latest decision to raise its benchmark rate by 25 basis points in September 2026, as well as the one 
to two additional hikes predicted  before the end of the year, we are entering a new interest -rate hiking cycle. This marks an 
important shift for investors, but higher policy rates do not necessarily imply weaker portfolio returns. What matters is why rates 
are rising, how quickly they increase, and whether economic growth can absorb tighter financial conditions. 
Our framework places the current environment in a healthy/high-yield regime. Growth remains resilient, credit spreads are 
tight and the yield curve is normalising. At the same time, the economy is approaching an important boundary. Inflation remains 
above target and the 10-year US government bond yield has reached 5%. A renewed rise in inflation or a sharper slowdown in 
activity could move markets into an unhealthy/high-yield regime. 
This argues for  measured risk-taking rather than an aggressively defensive stance . We maintain a slight overweight in 
equities, hold a strong overweight in gold and a neutral allocation to fixed income. Diversification remains key near a regim e 
boundary, and hedge funds can provide useful sources of return that are less dependent on the direction of traditional markets.

Not all interest-rate hiking cycles are the same

Traditional market 
analysis often treats 
rising rates as uniformly 
negative. History 
suggests a more 
nuanced conclusion.  
We classify hiking 
cycles along two 
dimensions: whether  
the increase in rates is 
healthy or unhealthy, 
and whether it occurs  
in a high- or low-yield 
environment. 
 Fig. 8 The different regimes of interest-rate hiking cycle 
Historical interest-rate hiking cycle defined by real growth and US 10-Year treasury yields

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; Since 1974

A healthy increase in rates is generally associated with firm 
demand, above -trend growth, resilient equities and stable 
credit markets. Central banks can move gradually and remain 
data-dependent because the economy is strong enough to 
withstand tighter fin ancial conditions. An unhealthy increase 
is more likely to be driven by supply constraints or persistent 
inflationary pressures. Growth slows, credit spreads widen, 
equities de-rate and central banks are forced to react more 
aggressively. 
The second dimension is the starting level of yields. In a high-
yield environment, bonds offer meaningful income and a 
greater cushion against moderate price declines. In a low -
yield environment, that protection is limited. This distinction 
matters because the same increase in yields can have very 
different portfolio consequences depending on the income 
available at the outset.

Assessing today’s market regime: Walking the 
fine line  
We assess the current environment as healthy/high yield 
(see Fig. 9), although it is moving closer to the boundary with 
an unhealthy/high-yield regime. Growth remains resilient, with 
2026 consensus GDP growth of around 2.1%, but it is below 
its longer-term trend. Inflation has eased , but remains above 
target, with headline CPI at 3.4% year -on-year in August 
2026, leaving the Fed focused on the balance between 
persistent price pressures and a potential growth slowdown. 
Market signals over the next 6-12 months remain broadly 
supportive. The 10-year US government bond yield is around 
5%, providing the highest bond carry since 2007, while the 
yield curve is re -steepening after its prolonged inversion.  
Equities remain positive, supported by AI -related earnings 
and soft-landing expectations, and high -yield credit spreads 
are near post-global-financial-crisis lows. 
Overall, resilient markets and benign credit conditions support 
the healthy classification. However, slower growth and above-
target inflation leave little room for disappointment. Further 
disinflation would help sustain the current regime, while sticky 
inflation, a rapid rise in yields or a sharp growth slowdown 
could push it into unhealthy territory.  
Higher rates, different outcomes 
Historical returns vary significantly across the four regimes 
(see Fig. 10), but history shows that some hiking regimes 
have generated solid returns across major asset classes. 
Unhealthy regimes are more complicated. Unhealthy/high -
yield periods (top-left corner) have historically still generated 
positive average returns, but these averages can conceal 
considerable volatility and dispersion. The most damaging 
combination has been unhealthy/low yield (as in 2022), when 
traditional 60/40 portfolios declined by an average of 15.8%. 
The lesson is that the economic cause of higher rates, and the 
level of yields, matter more than the label "rate hike" alone. 
Fig. 10 Historical returns by hiking-cycle regime  
Average returns for equities, bonds, Gold and a 60% 
equity/40% bond portfolio during rate-hiking periods. 
Hiking-cycle regime 60/40  Equity  Bonds  Gold

World and global aggregate benchmarks, Standard Chartered 
Why equities can coexist with 5% bond yields 
The absolute level of bond yields does not, by itself, determine 
equity performance. Investors must also consider the 
direction, speed and underlying cause of the move. 
When yields rise gradually because growth and 
investment demand are strong, corporate earnings 
growth can offset pressure on valuation multiples.  By 
contrast, a rapid increase driven by an inflation shock or 
disorderly repricing is more difficult for equities to absorb. 
Historically, several high -yield episodes combined positive 
real growth with resilient equity markets, while inflation-driven 
episodes, such as in 2022, were more challenging. 
The current level of yields has also been substantially 
incorporated into market prices. Investors have had time to 
adjust discount rates, equity valuations and financing 
assumptions to a world in which the 10-year Treasury yield is 
around 5%. This does not eliminate risk, but it means high 
yields are no longer a wholly new shock. The greater  threat 
would be a further rapid and unexpected increase in yields. 
AI investment sits at the centre of today’s market dynamic. 
Heavy spending on data centres, semiconductors, power 
generation and digital infrastructure is supporting earnings 
and helping equities remain resilient, despite elevated yields. 
At the same time, AI-related capital demand is competing with 
rising government borrowing and bond issuance. If 
productivity gains and earnings growth ultimately justify the 
investment, higher yields and stronger equities can coex ist.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

However, if bond supply outpaces available savings or AI 
returns disappoint, yields could rise more sharply and put 
pressure on valuations. The balance between productive 
investment demand and expanding sovereign debt supply 
remains a key market theme. 
Translating the hiking-cycle regimes and 
yields level into portfolio positioning 
Our positioning reflects a healthy/high -yield regime, while 
recognising we are close to the boundary with a less 
favourable environment. Historically, this regime has 
supported positive returns across equities, bonds, gold and 
60/40 portfolios. However, pe rformance has varied 
considerably depending on whether yields were rising, stable 
or falling (see Fig. 11), reinforcing the case for selective risk -
taking and broad diversification. 
Fig. 11 Asset average returns under different high-yield 
scenarios 
Historical average performance of major asset classes 
across different path of bond yields.

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
We maintain a slight overweight in equities, focused on US 
AI-related stocks and Asia ex -Japan. Equities performed 
particularly well when high yields were stable, historically 
returning 15.1% on average, but struggled when yields rose. 
Our moderate overweight , therefore, allows participation in 
resilient growth and earnings while limiting exposure to a 
renewed rate shock. 
We hold a strong overweight in gold, which performed well 
in both rising- and stable-yield environments and delivered the 
highest return of any asset when high yields were stable. Our 
fixed-income allocation is neutral . Although today’s yields 
offer attractive income, bonds have historically been 
vulnerable when yields were rising and underperformed when 
yield were stable. 
Diversification beyond a traditional 60/40 portfolio 
remains important  at this inflection point. Hedge funds 
generated positive returns across all regimes  - rising, stable 
and falling high -yield periods  - making them a useful 
complement when the direction of rates and the path between 
healthy and unhealthy regimes is uncertain.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): We have slightly reduced the probability of a soft landing from 50% by raising 
the odds of a ‘no -landing’. The global economy has  picked up steam this summer, powered by AI investments in the US and 
Asia. This is reflected in robust upgrades to corporate earnings estimates  and PMIs. Fiscal easing in Germany and Japan is 
supporting growth. Meanwhile, inflation has picked up amid higher energy prices due to a resurgen t Middle East conflict. As a 
result, the Fed turned hawkish, hiking rates for the first time since 2023. It was joined by the ECB and BoJ – the first time all 
three major central banks hiked rates in the same month. We expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the 
BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, disinflation should return by next year as energy and tariff effects fade, enabling 
the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. Meanwhile, China is likely to accelerate fiscal spending to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We raised the probability from 25% amid an acceleration in the AI-driven investment 
growth. If oil prices ease, US tax cuts, an AI -fuelled stock market boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan could boost 
“animal spirits” further. A global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also spur global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): This tail risk scenario includes 15% chance of a stagflation unfolding if geopolitical conflicts 
worsen and oil stays above USD 120/bbl. We also assign a 10% chance to a recession, potentially caused by a more rapid pace 
of rate hikes to quell inflation, a stock market downturn negating the wealth effect, or a bond selloff due to fiscal concerns.

Key chart  
We expect the Fed to hike 
rates twice more, the ECB to 
deliver another insurance 
hike, and the BoJ to hike 
once a quarter until June 
2027 to counter near-term 
inflation pressures. 
 Fig. 12 More rate hikes until June 2027, before cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures fade 
Our policy rate outlook*; oil price and US, Euro area, German near-term, long-term inflation

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *dotted lines reflect our expected rates path over the next 12m

Policy rates watch  
Fed turns hawkish to build credibility as oil reignites 
inflation; likely to hike twice more by June 2027. The US 
economy appears resilient, with consensus estimates for 
2026 GDP clustering around 2.1% for both 2026 and 2027. 
The main engine is the AI investment boom and hyperscaler 
datacentre buildout, reinforced by easy financial conditions 
and healthy private-sector balance sheets. The robust outlook 
was reflected in August’s PMI data, with both manufacturing 
and service sector confidence beating expectations. 
The labour market has picked up  this year , with average 
monthly payrolls of 80 ,000 vs. 10 ,000 in 2025 . The 
unemployment rate at 4.1%  suggests full employment, and 
the workweek lengthened in August. Offsetting vulnerabilities 
include a low savings rate, fading fiscal support, soft real  
personal spending and oil prices close to USD 100/bbl. 
Inflation remains a challenge. Core PCE inflation has run 
above 3% every month since March, and the hawkish case 
rests on supercore inflation remaining around 3 %, raising 
concerns about energy cost pass-through. Nevertheless, 
August's firm inflation print came from airfares, hotels and a 
record jump in wireless plans  (which is idiosyncratic) , while 
shelter cooled and core goods rose just 0. 7% y/y. Core 
consumer inflation at 2. 4% y/y is the lowest since 2021, and 
BEA methodology changes in end-September should 
mechanically shave 0.2-0.3pp off historical core PCE inflation. 
Following the unanimous Fed vote to hike in September, and 
still elevated inflation, we pencil in one more rate hike this 
year, followed by another 25bps hike to 4.5% by June 2027. 
This pace is slower than market pricing of another c. 90bps of 
hikes in the next 12 months. However, we expect disinflation 
to resume next year as the impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. 
This should create space for the Fed to cut rates by Q3 2027.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 11

Global Market Outlook 11 
ECB to hike once more: Euro area activity has proved more 
resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 growth estimate 
has been revised up to 0.9%, from 0.5% as recently as July, 
with ECB staff projections upgraded to  1.4% for 2027 and 
1.5% for 2028. Manufacturing PMIs have improved to 52.7 
regionally and services PMI to 53.0, helped by German fiscal 
delivery and falling household savings rates. A softer Q3 looks 
weather-driven, due to heatwaves and low Rhine water levels, 
though higher energy prices and real yields still cap demand. 
Headline inflation hit a near three -year high of 3.3% y/y in 
August and may peak around 3.4 -3.6% in Q4 2026 before 
easing through 2027. Underlying pressures look calmer: core 
inflation drifted down to 2.4%, services eased, and negotiated 
wage growth slowed to 2.4% y/y in Q2, down from a peak of 
5.6% in 2024 . Long -term inflation expectations remain 
anchored with no evidence of second -round effects. Inflation 
risks are two -sided – oil above USD 100/bbl and gas above 
EUR 70/MWh are fuelling near -term inflation, which in turn 
should hurt demand, driving inflation below 2% by H2 2027. 
The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.50% in September, the 
top of neutral estimates. We see scope for one more rate hike 
to 2.75% by year -end before a pause in H1 2027. As such 
pricing of 95bps more tightening toward an above-3% terminal 
rate appears too hawkish. We expect easing of energy-driven 
price pressures to allow for ECB rate cuts by Q3 2027. 
China to boost fiscal spending to support growth. China 
is entering a stabilisation phase in which production is firming, 
but the recovery remains narrow. The official manufacturing 
PMI rose to 49.8 in August from 49.2, with new orders, export 
orders and production back in expansionary territory, while 
export growth accelerated to 25.0% y/y. Momentum, however, 
is concentrated in high -tech and export manufacturing: retail 
sales, fixed-asset investment and credit growth remain soft, 
and the property downturn has yet to bottom. Full -year 2026 
growth should land near 4.5 –4.6%, broadly in line with the 
official 4.5–5% target, as domestic demand stays weak. 
Reflation is under way, but shallow and cost-driven rather than 
demand-led. Headline consumer inflation edged up to 0.8% 
y/y in August and producer inflation to 3.8%, lifted by higher 
commodity and energy prices and by official efforts to curb 
excessive price-cutting. Full-year inflation is expected around 
0.8–1.0%, against roughly zero in 2025, with producer 
inflation swinging from −2.6% to about +2.5%. The persistent 
supply-demand imbalance should cap the reflation impulse. 
Policy settings remain supportive, but with no rush to 
stimulate. Fiscal policy takes the primary role via faster 
spending and utilisation of bond proceeds, with broad fiscal 
spending potentially accelerating in H2 versus a sharp 
contraction in H1, and the augmented fiscal deficit widening 
modestly. Monetary policy plays a complementary role: a 
25bps cut in bank reserve requirements is likely, while the 
policy rate should stay unchanged. Slow public financing 
raises the likelihood of further policy fine-tuning this autumn. 
BoJ to hike once a quarter until Q2 2027. Japan's economy 
is proving more resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 
growth estimate has been revised up over the past month to 
0.8% from 0.6%. Exports rose 19% y/y in August, a twelfth 
consecutive y/y growth, while core machinery and machine 
tool orders indicate higher borrowing costs have yet to dent 
corporate investment appetite. Real wages climbed 2.4% y/y 
in July, the strongest since May 2021. The weak link is the 
household sector: spending continued to contract y/y, leaving 
an unusual two -speed economy in which external and 
corporate demand carry growth while domestic demand lags. 
Underlying inflation sits close to the 2% target, with pressure 
building upstream rather than from demand. Energy and 
commodity prices have risen sharply and earlier yen 
weakness has amplified import costs, with producer inflation 
running well ahead of CPI . The 10-year breakeven inflation 
has risen close to May’s record high of 2.3% amid growing risk 
of pass-through from producer to consumer prices.  
Bank of Japan Governor Ueda, after raising rates by 25bps to 
1.25% in September, alluded to rising inflation pressures, 
stating the policy environment has shifted to a “new phase”. 
He added that the BoJ must now act to prevent inflation from 
overshooting, rather than simply trying to reach the 2% target. 
September’s BoJ decision came in a 7-2 split vote, with two 
members nominated by Prime Minister Takaichi calling for a 
hold. However, Ueda’s post -decision comments lead us to 
believe that the BoJ board will be comfortable with a 25bps 
rate hike every quarter until Q2 2027, taking the rate to 2%.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

Foundation: Fixed income assets are a  core holding (Neutral) in our portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an 
Overweight stance on EM USD government bonds and an Underweight stance on DM government bonds.  
The Fed’s decision to hike rates in the past week confirms our expectations of a central bank willing to establish its independence 
against political pressure. The Fed's moderately hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. We expect Fed 
to hike twice, one by year-end and another hike in H1 2027 to  bring the Fed funds rate to 4.5%. The price reaction in the US 
government bond yield curve le ads us to believe the short -to-middle part of the curve ha s factored in most of the rate 
expectations. We now prefer to add positions in the 3-7-year bond maturity bucket. As oil price moderates in H2 2027, we see 
room for the Fed to contemplate rate cuts. That would also enable 10-year yields to come down, alongside the front end of the 
curve. Meanwhile, solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep yield premiums tight, even if the scope 
for further compression is limited. In EM, we continue to favour EM USD over local currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on European bank additional tier-1 (AT1) bonds (contingent convertibles [CoCos1]; FX-
hedged), US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, US utilities corporate hybrids, broad US HY and AUD corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We are Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, the 
Fed's policy pivot this month followed Chair Warsh's hawkish 
Jackson Hole speech and recent economic data prints, 
including a pick-up in job creation and inflation over the summer 
and a sharp rebound in oil prices due to the re-escalation of the 
Middle East conflict. Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike 
rates this week, we expect two more hikes by June 2027 on the 
back of robust growth amid strong AI investments and a 
balanced labour market, keeping inflation elevated.  
Over the next few months, we expect the 2-, 10- and 30-year 
US government bond yields to remain rangebound, with the 
10-year yield trading in the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons 
for this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially 
rising further due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle 
East; 2) AI capex plans showing no signs of slowing down, 
which continues to drive above -trend growth in the economy 
and AI -related heavy bond issuance. While short -covering 
trades could move bond yields lower temporarily as net-short 
positions in US government bond futures have reached 
extreme levels, we think such downshifts would be short-lived. 
By H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate and the 
inflationary effects from tariffs to fade, allowing the Fed to 
contemplate rate cuts. US 30 -year yields, on the other hand, 
are expected to remain elevated as the US fiscal deficit 
remains substantial next year. In addition, we do not think the 
so-called ‘Bessent put’  - expanding purchases of long -dated 
bonds, while skewing new issuance towards shorter 
maturities - will be effective as this does not address the 
fundamental issues  of rising debt levels . These factors are 
likely to keep the 'term premium' elevated. 
In the Euro area and Japan, the ECB and BoJ also hiked rates 
this month. It is the first time on record all three - Fed, ECB 
and BoJ - have hiked in the same month, as they respond 
primarily to a resurgence in energy -driven inflation amid tight 
job markets. BoJ is expected to continue hiking once every 
quarter till mid -2027 as we believe the bank  is behind the 
curve and will need to speed up the pace of rate hikes.

Global Market Outlook 13 
DM corporates – Core holding  
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across developed market credit. Yield premiums have 
bounced but remain close to historical lows, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability, and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Fig. 16 AI issuance challenges IG technical backdrop 
AI issuance (total & share of total), US IG gross issuance

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
particularly in the IG space, where heavy issuance related to 
AI continues to weigh on the long end of the curve. AI -driven 
supply is approaching USD 220bn for 2026  in US IG  and 
accounting for an increasingly significant share of gross 
issuance and crowding out other long-dated maturity bonds. 
We maintain a preference for selectivity at the long end, 
favouring the front to belly of the curve ( 3-7 years maturity) 
where risk -adjusted carry remains most durable. Issuance -
driven concessions in sectors with strong capital expenditure 
cycles (notably technology, capital goods, and basic 
industries) present opportunities for investors who can 
stomach near term volatility, especially when fund flows have 
remained robust: so far this year, high grade funds have seen 
cumulative inflows of ~8% of AUM. 
Fig. 17 DM IG spreads have bounced lately, but remain 
near historical lows 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
As an income proposition, DM HY continues to be well 
supported, with all-in yields in the mid -7% range and default 
rate still below trend for US HY in 2026. The shorter duration 
profile of HY, combined with its lower sensitivity to term 
premium and rate volatility, makes it an attractive sleeve in the 
current phase of the cycle. Despite experiencing net outflow 
YTD, HY demand has recovered from a net outflow position 
in the past 1-2 months, underscoring broad investor appetite 
for carry strategies even as spreads remain tight. We remain 
attentive to evolving rating migration trends and sector -
specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades slow and 
new issuance clusters around key theme s. However, we  
continue to see HY bonds as core holding allocations driven 
by healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics. 
Fig. 18 DM HY spreads are near historical lows as well 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight USD, 
core holding for local currency (LCY) bonds 
We re tain an  Overweight position in EM USD government 
bonds and retain EM LCY government bonds as a Core 
holding, reflecting a balanced perspective across both 
regional performance and currency dynamics. In EM USD 
sovereign debt, periods of spread widening have been brief  
and shallow, with attractive carry drawing sustained demand 
amid resilient global growth and stable external conditions. 
Issuance patterns remain front -loaded across many regions, 
reinforcing supportive technicals; relatively light investor 
positioning con tributes to contained risk of unwinding and 
limits market impact during episodes of moderate outflow. 
Regional nuances are increasingly notable. Latin America’s 
sovereign debt has tended to outperform, supported by more 
proactive monetary policy responses and firmer external 
balances. In Asia, higher -rated issuers offer valuable buffer 
during bouts of glob al rate volatility, while select frontier 
exposures within Africa and EMEA remain more sensitive to 
liquidity shifts and changes in risk appetite. Index composition 
also shapes resilience: the EM USD benchmark is less 
exposed to oil price shocks as non -oil importers represent a 
large share of exposure compared to the LCY universe. These 
features are particularly important as spreads across EM USD 
sovereigns hover near historically tight levels, leaving limited 
room for error. We expect returns to be primarily driven by 
carry rather than further spread compression.

### Page 15

Global Market Outlook 15

Our view

We are Overweight global equities as we expect solid earnings growth to drive the market higher . The wave of central 
bank rate hikes in September represent a near-term headwind to equity valuations, but these have been largely priced into 
markets by the rise in bond yields year-to-date. We expect earnings growth to offset valuation headwinds. We are Overweight 
US and AxJ equities, where AI adoption continues to drive growth in the technology and adjacent sectors. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, with good visibility on AI-driven growth. We lower China to a Core holding, 
as the valuation re-rating potential appears delayed. Within AxJ, we maintain core allocations to South Korea and India, and we 
are Underweight on ASEAN equities. 
We maintain a Core allocation to Japan, as nominal growth supports broad earnings revision, and a Core allocation to Europe 
ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings inflexion. We remain Underweight UK equities, which lacks exposure to growth sectors.

8 Equity – at a glance

Sundeep Gantori, CFA, CAIA 
Chief Investment Officer, Equities 
Fook Hien Yap 
Senior Investment Strategist 
Cindy Lam, CFA 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 16

Global Market Outlook 16

Expansion at a solid pace  
With economic activity expanding at a solid pace , we expect 
earnings growth to fundamentally drive global equities higher. 
Heading into 2027, rising AI adoption continues to drive 
strong growth  in the technology sector. Recent debates 
about regulating the pace of model developments could 
influence the mix of AI spending, with differing impacts on 
various parts of the AI value chain. However, rising adoption 
and growth in AI use cases should support massive growth in 
capex, benefitting the semiconductor industry, cloud service 
providers and internet platforms. The capex benefits also 
spillover to other industries providing the power infrastructure, 
materials and financing of the investmen ts. The risks are a 
slowdown in AI capex or net job losses from AI disruption, but 
we do not see these having a significant impact yet. 
Rate hikes  from central banks represent a headwind for 
equity valuations, although this has been largely priced in by 
bond markets, given the rise in yields we have seen this year. 
Further rate hikes are being priced by the market. However, 
we expect these headwinds to be offset by solid earnings 
growth in global equities: at 34% in 2026 and 16% in 2027.  
Fig. 21 Global equities continue to be supported by 
strong earnings growth, led by the technology sector  
Earnings growth by sectors for MSCI AC World index

Source: Company reports, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have softened this year  as strong 
earnings growth have outpaced the rise in the market. The 
12m forward P/E of around 20x remains at a premium to other 
markets but justified, in our view, by the high returns on equity, 
differences in sector composition and superior growth outlook. 
In the run-up to US mid-term elections in November, we would 
not be surprised to see higher volatility, though we would use 
any undue corrections as opportunities to add exposure. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ enjoys the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key 
beneficiary of the AI capex cycle. AxJ also has the lowest 
P/E valuation, given the earnings surge ha s not yet been  
reflected in the market. Within AxJ, we are Overweight 
Taiwan, which benefits from clear visibility on AI -driven 
growth and dominance in manufacturing advanced AI chips. 
We lower China to a Core holding as the valuation re -
rating potential now appears delayed. Share placements for 
AI spending will likely continue, diluting earnings. Regulatory 
concerns still linger from US restrictions on AI chips and 
distillation accusations, even as the Trump-Xi summit signals 
a mutual desire for steady ties. Looming offshore trust taxes 
may lead to further sell down pressures , while domestic 
demand remains weak. However, valuation s remain 
inexpensive with rapid tech innovation and AI -localisation 
investments. Policy stimulus could provide upside. 
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are 
positive on the long-term agreements giving greater earnings 
visibility on memory chip s. Positioning is also cleaner, with 
reduced foreign investor exposure and declin ing leveraged 
ETFs. India remains a Core holding amid positive earnings 
revision and strong domestic demand , alongside  macro 
headwinds from RBI rate hikes. We remain Underweight on 
ASEAN, as oil shocks and a supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 22 Global equity valuations have declined this year, 
with market gains led by earnings growth 
Composition of returns YTD for regional MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Standard Chartered. Min-Max over last 10 years 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal economic growth is supporting solid 
broad-based earnings revisions , with rising rates 
supportive of bank earnings. The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is also pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings inflection  after three 
stagnant years, with rising rates supportive of banks’ 
earnings. Fiscal expansion is benefitting domestic demand 
and the industrial sector, although earnings growth in the 
region still lags that of the US and AxJ.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK , 
making it lag the performance of other markets.

Global Market Outlook 17

Add Global Semiconductors, remove HSTECH 
We initiate d an Opportunistic Idea on Global 
Semiconductors on 9 Sep t 2026. We expect sustained AI 
investments and rising AI adoption to support earnings 
growth, with demand widening beyond processors to memory, 
networking, and advanced manufacturing equipment . 
Valuations have also eased since the middle of the year. 
We remove Hang Seng Technology (31 Oct 2024 to 24 Sep 
2026: -1.2%) amid mixed earnings trends, while AI 
monetisation has yet to keep pace with rising investments. 
Widening US-China tech restrictions adds to the uncertainty, 
and we see better risk-adjusted opportunities elsewhere.
